- Third review of check-run plan v3 (fcd86380a): REJECT, 6 blockers. (1) sourceHash byte-identity impossible — sync hashes cover different objects, sync rows are id-ordered vs the run's group-tuple order, and above 10k sync rows is []. (3) amount_totals in plain header columns under tenant_read IS the payroll disclosure §2.3 claims to prevent (one currency total = one employee's net pay) and T14 cannot see it. (4) loadOwnedCapturedEvidence takes FOR SHARE on captured_documents before the storage read, so in-txn verification holds up to 100k row locks for minutes and one contended document aborts the run (retryable → burns all 3 attempts). (5) assertEffectivePermission under authMethod='job' reloads+decrypts the delegation per call, i.e. ~500k queries and 100k decrypts per run; effectivePermissionQueries.ts is not in the owned set. (6) The fan-out collides on UNIQUE(company_id,requester_person_id,kind,idempotency_key). Majors incl. bank reviewState is population-global; duplicates drops the Sage import population; the differential gates assert fields that diverge by construction and the over-cap test has no oracle; the purge function lets any member destroy sealed evidence; the systemd timeout cannot be kind-aware because the spawn path never receives the kind. - MCP branch COMPLETE at 5e6009c97 (24 commits, base c47c7e246). Round 5 finished the four outstanding tools: payroll previews (two seeded runs with calculation receipts, employees, components, periods and GL accounts under a payroll_run policy) returned previews while both live calls refused — now they ask payrollApprovalRequired first; bank allocation and reversal previews returned would_post:1 / would_reverse:1 under a payment policy — now answered by amount, because a bank allocation executes against a control intent THE WRITE ITSELF CREATES, so a preview cannot materialise it. The kernel gained a preview-safe form that runs the material question inside its own savepoint and falls back to "does any policy exist" when the operation cannot be materialised, so a preview never answers "no approval needed" merely because the question failed (also protects against a poisoned transaction). Conservative by design: a preview may refuse where the live call would have allowed, never the reverse. Mutations red for both new guards. bulkApproveRunner split at the real seam (partition 204 lines, posting 137).
- RESPONSE SHAPE CHANGES for MCP clients: bulk tools keep their fields but move approval-bound documents from would_post into skipped[] with a reason naming request_operation_approval; approve_pay_run, post_payroll_journal and reverse_allocations now return an ERROR ENVELOPE instead of a preview object, as do settle_by_contra, close_fiscal_period, reopen_fiscal_period, year_end_close and the two bank-line cash tools when authority is missing. - Impersonation round 4 (head 88f5aea0b): P1 CLOSED — a proof now carries the stashId it belongs to and is honoured only while that exact stash row lives and no impersonation_end/impersonation_end_failed row names its session, so deleting the stash (what ending does) kills it instantly and every proof minted before this change is refused outright. RED through real withAuth: after the stash is deleted the same cookies returned 200 at bac17ce2f and now return 401; m23 red. GoTrue corollary MEASURED against the real server with a disposable probe user: sign in → /user 200; logout scope=global → 204; the same unexpired access token → /user 403; refresh → 400. So a revoked session IS rejected by the /user fallback on this deployment, closing the dropped-proof path — recorded as a GoTrue-version property to re-measure after any upgrade. P3s closed: platform-admin context is identified POSITIVELY via the platform-import request registry, not by absence of a principal — the naive version swept in invite/extension tokens, MCP persistence and the audit worker and broke six tests, caught and fixed; end-impersonation records the revoked session id and scope, writes impersonation_end_failed when GoTrue refuses (and that row itself kills the proof), and returns a warning when it had to revoke every session the member owns; commentary now records and projects approved_by_impersonator, rejected_by_impersonator, withdrawn_by_impersonator and rejected_by (nothing recorded the rejecter before). 211 integration / 16 files with one pre-existing failure identical at base; units 10,927 with the five known pre-existing failures; tsc/eslint clean; 11 mutations all caught.
- BREAKING, HEIN'S CALL: sageMigrationCommitService can no longer approve a tenant's supplier invoices under a platform-admin session, so the Sage migration commit now STOPS at the approval step. The agent flagged it rather than papering over it. The remedy is to give that path the confined historical-import treatment (a platform import target with its own approval), or to decide the migration commit may approve as the platform principal — which is precisely the self-approval shape this work exists to prevent.
- Background security review flagged independentReview.ts as a fail-open authorization gate; ACCEPTED, not covered by round 4. assertIndependentRequestReview returns early when there is no store and again when a store carries no controlPrincipal and is not positively identified as platform-admin, so the default for a principal-less REQUEST context is allow and only one case is refused — a new auth path, a middleware ordering change or any bug leaving controlPrincipal unset silently bypasses segregation of duties with nothing failing visibly. Fix dispatched: invert the polarity so that inside a request context every legitimately principal-less caller (invite and extension tokens, the audit worker, MCP persistence) is identified POSITIVELY the way platform-admin now is, and anything matching none of them is refused; the no-store path (script or background task) stays the only blanket allow and is made explicit. Required evidence: the full list of allowed principal-less request contexts with how each is identified and why it cannot be forged, a test per allowed context, and one proving an unrecognised principal-less request context is refused — the regression guard for the whole class.
- UUID boundary branch FINAL at ac95e4b3d (23 commits on 96ef68022). The silent dispatch failure was fixed by REMOVING the special case rather than logging inside it: recordFailedDispatch no longer takes an expected state and records the state observed under FOR UPDATE — ready means never dispatched, attempting means an attempt lost the race — so every claim failure leaves a true record and the earlier "do not record Release already claimed" instruction became unnecessary. The author first tried the log-line route and abandoned it ON EVIDENCE: the warn fires with the right payload but vi.spyOn(log,'warn') does not intercept it from the integration suite, so the assertion would have been vacuous; a durable event is both stronger and assertable. Mutations red: fixed status instead of observed (2), no claim record (2), no ack record (1). Lock ordering: no clean fix without a migration — recordCommittedChange writes control_entity_versions while withInvoiceMatchSourceLocks holds SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE on it, so the dispatch pool's 5s statement_timeout can lose the record exactly when a release needs it; bounded by the holder's own 2s lock_timeout and by holding only for its own transaction. A retry was declined (it would not close the window and would double the observer's tail latency); the degradation is instead made greppable by logging the PostgreSQL error code beside reason and action. FOLLOW-UP RECORDED: the real fix is an append-only release-event table with no version lineage, which this change does not carry.
- CI GAP stated for delivery, in the branch's own words: this branch's central evidence does NOT run in `bun run ci` and a green gate must not be read as covering it. The whole tests/integration tree is DATABASE_URL-gated and outside the gate; all 129 tests across 17 files, every RED→GREEN and every mutation, live there; arPostingReceiptUuid.int.test.ts THROWS without ISAFLOW_UUID_BOUNDARY_BASELINE, so it cannot pass by accident and cannot run unattended; and the suites must be run deliberately, serially (--no-file-parallelism, since parallel runs deadlock in seedReleaseSupplier), under flock with the fixture env and baseline worktree variables set.
- Blind review of the MCP branch (c47c7e246..5e6009c97): NO P0/P1. Verified: the registry↔inventory test derives from MCP_TOOLS and fails with a named diff; write:audit is enforced twice and the ONLY two enqueue entry points (the three tools and the comparisons route) are both gated, with no cron/worker/delegation caller; controlledOperationApprovalRequired returns the character-for-character live condition from the same loadOperationMaterial; withSavepoint fails closed on 25P02 via markTxUnusable; the amount form returns true on ANY aggregate_window_days before evaluating, so an aggregate policy cannot fool it, and the amount it passes is provably the binding amount; loadOperationMaterial writes nothing. BOTH retractions CONFIRMED: perform_bank_reconciliation imports only the six non-posting functions, and withGlMergeChanges asserts write:accounts + write:post and re-asserts per changed account. P2s to fix: the preview fallback cannot distinguish "policy question failed" from "cannot be materialised", so a receiptless payroll run is told an approval is required and the agent will request one, retry and fail again for the real reason it was never told — contradicting the tool's own description; four tools now assert write:locks on the preview while declaring a different scope, so the registry scope gate never fires and the caller gets the generic failure with no route to the answer (every pre-existing connection hits this); and lock_vat_period is the LAST write:true tool under a read:* scope, defended only by the assert added this round — the posture the audit tools had before the fix — while the new test proves agreement, not correctness, so re-introducing the original bug passes CI if the JSON is regenerated. The missing one-line invariant (every write:true tool carries a write:* scope) is worth more than the agreement test. P3s: reverse_allocations' description still promises a preview it now refuses; the fallback catch swallows every error with no log; bankPreviewApproval defaults a missing amount to 0, answering "no approval required" against a greater_than policy; previews now hold the tenant control-policy advisory lock to end of transaction, new contention that did not exist before.
- merge_gl_accounts, reviewer's judgement: agrees with the direction, corrects the premise — a gl_account_merges row with moved_line_ids and digest plus per-account and per-journal change events ALREADY exist; what is missing is the approval half (no policy binding, no effect row, no idempotent replay, no exposure reservation). The blocker is design, not plumbing: the operation's material is the set of journal lines it will repoint, which includes every line in an OPEN period, so any ordinary posting between request and execution invalidates a line-set binding — which is why the code re-reads and asserts population equality instead. Recommended cheap shape: bind the INTENT (company, source, target, blocked flag, source and target account versions), gate it, and write an effect row keyed on the merge record id, leaving the line population under the existing equality assert — about a day. Full line-set binding is a week and should not start until someone answers the stability question. HEIN'S CALL to schedule.
- Review of impersonation round 4 (bac17ce2f..88f5aea0b): five P2s. (1) end-impersonation.ts:62-64 records the CALLER's session id, which on the global path is the ADMIN's restored session — so impersonationDelegationLive, which matches details->>'target_session_id' against the IMPERSONATED session, stays live on the 502 path where the stash is deliberately retained, and the copied cookies keep passing the AAL2 bypass for the rest of the hour: the P1 is still open on that path. The success path also stores a key named target_session_id holding the admin's id — a falsified audit row on an evidence branch. Neither path is tested. (2) The sentinel narrowing reintroduces an attacker-controlled fail-open: on a database lagging 675 an admin impersonating a member with NO verified MFA factor deletes the proof cookie, verifyToken passes, and approvals are recorded under the member's name with no impersonator at all. Correct remedy is to assert the reader once at startup/deploy-gate and keep the request path fail-closed. Also resolveImpersonatingActor is called only in withAuth.ts:163, so any approver path outside withAuth is unmarked — a standing invariant. (3) Census still short, and the biggest gap is the strongest approver of record in the product: afsReviewService.ts:152/191/~270 (reviewed_by, signed_off_by, locked_by) are unguarded, so an admin impersonating a partner can sign off and lock annual financial statements with no refusal and no record; afsSectionService.ts:84-86 takes those names as caller-supplied input, a second door on the same table; payroll/taxDirectiveService.ts:176 likewise. timeTrackingService exclusion confirmed legitimate. (4) Commentary can still persist the sentinel verbatim into the impersonator columns — a text column meant for a user id holding a magic string that joins to nothing and reads to an auditor as an actor. (5) Sage AP migration fails 100% SILENTLY as a counts summary; the remedy is to route sage-commit through withPlatformImportTarget (which exempts itself by installing a controlPrincipal with a null impersonator), and the routing must land WITH the guard or the guard must be gated until it exists. P3s: the liveness query sequentially scans admin_audit_log twice per proof-carrying request (target_id::text and an unindexed details key) on a table that grows forever; the app pool's SELECT grant on admin_impersonation_stash is unverified and a missing grant would silently refuse every proof with no test able to tell it from "live"; migration 676 was amended after creation (checksum-conflict pattern if already applied to isaflow_dev) and its text columns force casts against a uuid approved_by; independentReview is no longer a leaf and now pulls the auth graph back into accounting. CLEARED: pre-change proofs refused outright, proof/stash cross-binding sound with no replay or clock-skew window beyond the stash row, positive platform-admin identification unforgeable and correctly scoped, the nine new guards all first-statement with no bulk/MCP bypass, and the six adjusted tests fixed rather than weakened.
- TO BE WRITTEN DOWN FOR HEIN, not fixed: the platform-import exemption is TOTAL — anything reachable inside a platform-import target context bypasses the independence guard entirely, including action 'execute'. The confinement relabels the platform administrator's approval authority rather than removing it.
- Review of the UUID boundary final commits (db24254d6..ac95e4b3d): no P0/P1. Independently verified that payment_release_outbox is NOT in the release binding's sourceVersions (only payment_preparation, supplier, payment_release_intent), so the extra version bump cannot brick an approval binding hash, and that the bump only lands on rows in attempting or terminal, which the 636 trigger can never return to ready. Callers unweakened: acknowledgement_failed is provably always attempting (the continuation alone holds the attempt_id under FOR UPDATE), and the claim path now always records where it previously refused — strictly more information. FIXES DISPATCHED: give recordFailedDispatch its own SET LOCAL statement_timeout='30000ms' (the reviewer's suggestion — the last-resort path deserves a longer tail than the 5s pool budget, and it costs nothing on the success path); correct three comments whose reasoning is wrong although the code is right (lock_timeout bounds ACQUISITION only, so the exposure is the holder's whole unbounded transaction rather than 2s; "takes no other lock" is false since the outbox row lock comes first, though the ordering conclusion holds; the greppable code is 57014, not 55P03, because the dispatch pool sets no lock_timeout); enumerate terminal states in the docblock and state that reconciliation keys off status, not action, since a delayed loser can write dispatch_failed against status exported; re-establish or retract the "spy does not intercept" claim, which does not survive reading the logger (log is a mutable module-scope singleton the service imports directly) and add the spy-free catch-path test via a trigger raising on the event insert; and route that log.error to Bugsink, since today it reaches stderr and nothing else for a payload meaning money may be gone with no record.
- Fallout FINAL (91b634db6 tests / 3d7846080 fix): the P1 was real and the author's own new test caught it before my message arrived — items:[] for an entity whose events were retained under another spelling. controlChangeHistoryService (all six predicates plus evidence joins), controlChangeHistoryPolicy and transactionEvidenceQueries now match lower(entity_id); version and event-version use max(...) so a fork reports the highest retained lineage instead of erroring in a scalar subquery; the evidence reader prefers the caller's spelling when both exist. M18 red. The three version readers now match lower(entity_id) and prefer the caller's spelling, so a legacy spelling EXTENDS the lineage instead of conflicting forever; the bank-rule test flipped from asserting a refusal to asserting the extension (v2 under the retained spelling, event under the same), M20 red, and the .claude.md line documenting the permanent block is gone. Census by consumer, each verified against head: audit snapshots (journals, bank, statements, attestations, AR/AP docs, payroll) reported effect and version null for a controlled entity — fixed with 2 tests; history and its access policy — fixed with a test; transaction evidence, risk projections (supplier invoice, supplier payment, journal, three-way match) and the payroll posting receipt (a posted run read as unposted) — fixed, consistency only, and the author says plainly they carry no dedicated test rather than implying coverage; bank child reversal guard paymentId arm and the transfer payload arms — fixed; exposure write plus aggregate sum — fixed with a test. M19 and M21 red.
- BACKFILL DECLINED on measured evidence (fixture database only): mixed-case rows are versions 275/65,759, events 356/118,128, evidence 357/118,145, effects 285/12,926, approvals 192/11,342, exposure 6/159 — with 200 VERSION FORKS (one entity, two spellings) and 391 version rows whose events already use a different spelling, so the link crosses spellings today. Canonicalizing control_entity_versions alone would neither break the link nor be needed given case-insensitive readers, and merging 200 forks would mean choosing a version number and discarding a material_hash pointing at retained evidence — adjudication, not a migration. ACCEPTED. The UNIQUE lower(entity_id) index was NOT added: it cannot build while forks exist and would convert a legacy fork into a permanent outage, so the narrow concurrent-baseline window stays open pending adjudication of the fork population — recorded as a follow-up. The trailing occurred_at DESC,id was dropped from the change-events index.
- SINGLE-SHOT OPERATION SET, assessment for Hein: the author supports it but says the proposed enumeration is incomplete in a way that matters — bank_allocation.reverse, cash_application.reverse and payroll_compensation.change are equally intent-scoped, so omitting them would make the set look like a policy when it is an accident of which domains were audited. The exclusions are right (fiscal_period.*, payroll_run.*, year.close and the cancel/void family address long-lived entities that legitimately accumulate effects). One entry must be checked before any code: recurring_invoice.generate, because if its entity is the schedule rather than the run, a blanket refusal breaks monthly generation outright — exactly the breakage a declarative set makes invisible. The gate must sit AFTER the kernel's committed-effect branch, or every legitimate retry stops replaying and starts refusing. Proof shape: table-driven refusal per single-shot operation whatever the operation_id and binding_hash, acceptance per excluded one, a census test asserting every operation in the vocabulary appears in exactly one list so a new operation cannot be added without choosing, and a replay test proving a retry still returns the original receipt.
- payment_release_outbox family gap: the author recommends opening it — events that are durable but unreadable are the same defect class as the P1 just fixed, evidence recorded where no tenant or auditor can see it, and the fix is small (add the family to the history vocabulary with a read:payments-style permission mapping).
- Results: PG snapshot 2/2, entry 10/10, exposure 3/3, history green; regression 799 passed across 114 files with four failures and four un-runnable files all reproduced identically at the branch base 80b5e2f57 in a throwaway worktree; units 10,937 passed / 3 failed, the same directPostBypassGuards cases that fail at base and touch an AR guard this branch never edited; tsc, typed ESLint and agent-docs:check all clean.
- Review of fallout final (b6348f429..3d7846080): P1 CONFIRMED and INTRODUCED BY THIS COMMIT — control_entity_versions PK makes a fork two rows, and every snapshot/risk join moved from an at-most-one exact match to lower(v.entity_id)=r.id::text, which fans out. counts() does not join versions but page() does, so one forked journal entry makes page() return two rows for one id and the capture throws "Snapshot source keyset did not advance" or "Incomplete snapshot source population": the whole audit snapshot job for that company dies permanently, against 200 measured forks. Risk queries additionally double-count and inflate their count(*) OVER() populations. Fix is SQL dedupe (LATERAL ... LIMIT 1 or DISTINCT ON) everywhere, and the reviewer's structural point is upheld: having declined the backfill, the dedupe is not optional. P2: a forked entity's history is now a plausible-looking MERGE of two lineages — max() for the version but both lineages' events, ordered by new_version with no tiebreaker and a cursor of new_version > lastVersion, so a page boundary between two events sharing a number silently drops the sibling; the doc still says two spellings fail closed while the code merges, so doc and code contradict. P2: the census missed a fourth self-reading version reader with the same defect (defaultAccountsControl.ts:29-31,46 — saving default accounts blocked forever for a legacy-spelled gl_account), plus auditSnapshotSageInvoiceQueries.ts:21, payrollControlQueries.ts:79, unappliedCashHistoryQuery.ts:38,41, historicalImportSourceKind.ts:21,24, controlledYearEnd.ts:32. P2: glAccountChangeControl.ts:24,27 and glMergeChangeControl.ts:72 pick a version by collation order or first match while changeEventService prefers the caller's spelling, so on a fork the version read and the row updated come from different lineages. P3s: payrollPostingReceipt is half-canonical (predicate lowers, comparisons stay exact); aggregateOperationPolicy's DISTINCT ON discards a possibly larger reserved exposure; 679's dropped index tail costs a sort in the query that wanted it. ROOT CAUSE OF THE WHOLE CLASS BEING INVISIBLE: no test seeds a fork — every new test uses one spelling — so fork fixtures across snapshot, risk and history are now required as the regression guard. Also noted: the open concurrent-baseline window matters MORE now, since a newly minted fork used to cost an understated version and would now cost a dead audit snapshot job.

- T1 SPIKE COMPLETE — GO against the 10-minute ceiling (spike branch, 62096e9d2, JSON at forensic-evidence/check-run-spike-100k.json; never to be merged). 100k journals run in ~102–116 s of the 600 s budget, repeated at load 18–27 (a run at load 40 was ~35% slower). Proposed journal_risk_checks limits, each traced to a measurement: maxRuntimeMs 300000 (M1+M6, ~2.6× the worst clean run), graceMs 30000 (M11 reclaim 57–119 ms), idleMs 15000 (longest inter-statement gap is sealing 100k findings ~2 s), fetchRows 1000 (2.3 ms p50, 4.5–8 ms p95; the whole sort lands on the first FETCH at 2.7–2.8 s), maxChildrenPerParent 1000 (per-query cost ~0.9 ms p50, extrapolated above the fixture's 150), maxSlabRows 1000 (real slabs ≤6 rows; analyseJournalRisks copies each group so cost grows with the square of slab size — 174 ms per 1000-row slab in a faulty first fixture), maxFindings 100000 (2.5 s insert, 124 MB WAL, 1.2 s verified re-read, +57–61 MB RSS; RLS WITH CHECK unmeasurable and M12 shows it genuinely runs), maxThresholds 100 (10 measured, linear, extrapolated). CONDITIONS on the GO: the lease must be set to maxRuntimeMs+graceMs at claim (the 90 s lease expired mid-M7); the check-run unit needs its own RuntimeMaxSec (65 s would kill every run; M11/M12 ran with no unit, so no runtime cap was in force, recorded in the JSON); permission must be asserted ONCE before the cursor (M5: ~10 ms and 4 queries per call under job auth, 8.7 ms of it decrypting the delegation — per-journal calls would add ~17 minutes at 100k); and batching child queries is recommended (per-journal change queries are 94% of run time, 95,239 queries; one per FETCH took 5.7–6.5 s). M2: the sort spills ~17 MB at default work_mem; SET LOCAL work_mem='64MB' removes it (the index is not chosen). M13: no locks on gl_journal_entries; a concurrent update finished in 3–9 ms. M9 gives no production number (no other writers) but shows xmin held for the full ~110 s.
- M7/M8 SETTLE THE EVIDENCE DESIGN for the deferred detectors: verify document bytes OUTSIDE the long transaction. Same throughput either way (75–79 vs 85–88 docs/s), but inside one RR transaction 20,000 FOR SHARE locks were held to COMMIT and a concurrent writer hit the 5 s lock_timeout in EVERY run; short per-slab RC transactions let it through in 5 ms and the hash re-check found 0 mismatches. ~11–12 ms per document is the permission reload inside loadOwnedCapturedEvidence, not storage. 20k documents ≈ 4 minutes either way.

Only anonymous volumes attached to no container could have been removed; named volumes and all existing containers' data survive, and the shared forensic database is up. It also set SKIP_SECRET_SCAN=1 on a commit that did not need it. Recorded as a standing rule in memory: agents remove only what they created, by exact name, never a machine-wide prune.

One lineage rule, retainedLineage, now drives readControlEntityVersion, recordCommittedChange, bank rules, GL account changes, GL merges and default accounts (the fourth reader), so the version read and the row extended are always the same lineage — the GL fork test hit "stale" and saving default accounts returned 500 before, both pass now. - DECISION, taken on principle after two rounds each finding a case the previous rule broke: ONE lineage rule for writers AND projections — follow the MOST ADVANCED lineage (highest retained version, tie broken canonical-first), because that is where the entity's current state and its version-keyed dependent rows actually live, and using the same rule on both sides keeps writer and snapshot in agreement. DETECTORS are the exception and pick no lineage at all: they aggregate change history across every retained spelling and flag the fork, so no fraud signal can be hidden by the spelling an edit was recorded under. The baseline proof becomes a recorded evidence artefact and leaves the suite.
- Review of platform round 7 (b7f911f71..c3e3896ce): all seven claims hold for the code touched (every verifyToken caller runs on the login role; the two test corrections were genuine, not weakened; the deploy gate's grep cannot block spuriously). But it found the AFS review problem much wider than restore — all PRE-EXISTING: the overrides endpoint and updateAfsSection keep a stale review after content changes; AI narrative accept can overwrite a SIGNED-OFF or FILED document with status unchanged; restore and overrides are bound only by section id and company, not to the URL's document, so a request against an editable document can REPLACE A FILED DOCUMENT'S CONTENT; invalidations are invisible to a partner. Plus P3s introduced by this branch: an unlocked-read race in restoreVersion, an audit insert after GoTrue revocation that can strand the admin in a 502 loop, a success message naming the wrong role (grepped by deploy.sh), and a comment-only edit to 677 that changes its checksum on the fixture DB.
- DECISION — SCOPE SPLIT: the pre-existing AFS review-integrity defects move to a new branch fix/afs-review-integrity-20260910 (based on c3e3896ce, which already carries the restore fix and the impersonation guards) with its own agent, so the platform branch finishes on its own scope and becomes integrable. Principle given to the new agent: a review is an attestation about specific content, so any content-changing write must invalidate it (drop to pending and clear reviewer/signatory fields); no write may touch a section of a locked document; every write must operate on the section belonging to the document named in the request. saveVersion's signed_off→reviewed demotion moves out of the snapshot function into each content-changing write. The management-pack status stays a separate follow-up.

- AFS round 2 complete at cd53d786f (c27391193 tests / 5a95f9f62 source + migration 681 / aba961aab / 02288a5b7 / 88b601b3a / cd53d786f docs). - MCP branch FINAL at 909fb0d4f (13 commits over b3fd8cce2). The six P3s closed, and item 4 uncovered a REAL PRE-EXISTING DEFECT: a caller-supplied bad approvalId surfaced as a 500 because migration 627's authorities function RAISEs with ERRCODE 42501 and that lookup was called OUTSIDE the savepoint on BOTH the preview and the live paths, aborting the request transaction so even a mapped 409 returned COMMIT_FAILED. Both lookups moved inside their savepoints; both paths mark a caller-supplied approval failure with the SQLSTATE preserved, and only P0001/42501 qualify so a deadlock or serialization failure still 500s; covered live and previewed over real HTTP, with mutations red for the route branch and for dropping 42501. period_range_mismatch — the outcome refusing all 13 production companies — now has a CI-runnable route test asserting 409, both date ranges and the month name. no_fiscal_period DELETED: the agent accepted the correction that its stated reason was wrong, showed the outcome is unconstructable (period_range_mismatch already proves a period ends on the closing date), and left the real behaviour, 'Closing fiscal period unavailable', as a 500 because it is a concurrency race like its sibling — declining to invent a friendly status for a concurrency fault; gl/.claude.md corrected and the mirror regenerated. The refund header is guarded by !res.headersSent so a refusal no longer logs a false 'flow refund failed'. The account-range intra-tenant split stays, with the reasoning recorded: the range constrains write:accounts only and read:accounts is unconstrained, so which of its own company's ids exist is already visible to that caller. Synthesised inputSchema:{} sites replaced by the real registry tools, with the two hardcoded refusal constants removed. tsc, eslint, agent-docs all clean; units 10,943 with the 3 pre-existing failures; components 453; integration 123 passed / 3 env-gated. Review of the savepoint round dispatched.
- Blind review of the AFS signing round (4c5e1b682..cd53d786f): transition locks and the webhook state machine hold up (no deadlock cycle with reorder; a refused document is not stuck). TWO P1s. (1) THE SIGNATURE DOES NOT ATTEST WHAT WAS SENT: send-for-signing posts bytes read from a pre-rendered HTML file while the fingerprint is computed independently from afs_sections, and NOTHING in the repo ever writes that file — only send-for-signing reads it and the new test writes it itself — so the artifact is of unknown provenance and arbitrary age; render v1, edit, re-sign-off, send, and signers see v1 while the fingerprint records v2 and the webhook accepts. (2) POST /generate BYPASSES THE ENTIRE GUARD: it refuses only signed_off/filed, so a document out for signature passes, and generateSection writes content, data_snapshot and status in raw SQL with no lock, no void and no audit row, leaving reviewed_by/signed_off_by populated so the section still reports as attested over content nobody reviewed — while both .claude.md files now assert the opposite invariant. P2s: migration 681 refuses EVERY in-flight signature on arrival (NULL fingerprint) unless backfilled or grandfathered; signing_refused_reason is read by nothing while the signatories DID sign, so an executed signature sits at the provider with no in-app record and is never voided; AI generate's re-check compares content while the rendered field is overrides, and it un-accepts a narrative without voiding, contradicting updateAfsSection on the same branch. P3s: reorder's localeCompare lock ordering can deadlock two clients using different UUID case; the wrong id in a not-found message; sort_order changes note numbering without voiding and a partial payload can duplicate orders; the note_templates join multiplies the mandatory count and reads the wrong framework; the webhook's FOR UPDATE has no lock_timeout; AI burns model budget before its 409.
- MY GATE WAS WRONG and is being changed: refusing reviewRequired:false pack generation would silently stop those companies' packs every month forever with only a log line. It will now GENERATE a draft and NOT deliver it, with a status the caller and scheduler can see, keeping the pack available to a human without asserting a signature nobody gave.
- Also required: the mutation list exists only in the agent's report, not in the branch — mutants, results and the redundancy argument for the three survivors go into the evidence doc so the claim can be checked without the author.

P2 CONFIRMED: marking on code===undefined catches EVERY plain Error in the region, so lockApproval's 'Approval material is invalid' — a stored-approval tamper detector that also fires after any canonicalisation change — and 'Unsupported controlled operation' and any TypeError now return a caller-facing 409 quoting the internal message, and the new branch returns before both log sites so an integrity trip is INVISIBLE to monitoring where the old path logged twice. All routed back as the last round.
- MCP round closed at `6ff2d43e4`. `SuppliedApprovalRefusal` is now a declared class thrown at the six caller-fixable sites, and `markSuppliedApprovalFailure` marks ONLY SQLSTATE P0001/42501 — so 'Approval material is invalid' (the stored-approval tamper detector), 'Unsupported controlled operation' and any TypeError go back to log.error + a 500 that never echoes the message, while a real refusal is answered 409 AND logged (log.warn with the year and reason) at both the mark site and the route. I verified this myself rather than on the report: read the class and the marking predicate at the named commit, ran the new suites (suppliedApprovalRefusal 5/5, glBypassYearRoute + handlerErrorReply + approvalReply 14/14), then MUTATED the predicate back to `code===undefined` and watched 2 tests go red, restoring the file afterwards. The 42501 three-way conflation is answered rather than silently accepted: telling a GUC fault from a bad approvalId needs a probe of app.current_company_id() AFTER the savepoint rollback (25P02 forbids probing in place), which the call shape cannot do; instead both sites log the SQLSTATE, so a context fault shows as a burst across callers and companies and a bad id as one caller's single id. fiscalPeriodCloseService has no REST caller that can supply an approvalId today, so the reachable half was fixed in handlerErrorReply.
- MERGE `51e7c6de1` (MCP -> integration) CONFLICTED in approvalExecutionService.ts exactly where the two branches both edited the kernel. Resolution keeps BOTH: integration's `assertPlatformImportOperation(ctx,ref,'execute')` and its three-argument `assertRequesterAuthority(ctx,row,authorities)` (needed for the platform-import requester path), plus MCP's `assertControlledOperationAuthority`, the savepointed authority lookup and `lockPolicyThenApproval`. - The merge rule earned another entry. Running the OTHER branch's suites found `effectivePermissionReasons.test.ts` failing with a TypeError, not an assertion: MCP's two new cases pass a bare `{}` as the ControlTx, and integration's kernel now reads `ctx.principal.authMethod` on the FIRST line of assertEffectivePermission. tests/** is outside tsconfig, so nothing typechecked the fixture — the same class as the untyped-fixture memory. Fixed in `6ac1c89c6` by giving those two contexts a principal; the three `directPostBypassGuards` failures are pre-existing on integration and unchanged by this merge.

The guard is a no-op unless authMethod is 'platform_import', so the exposure is narrow — a platform-import principal could preview a year-end or fiscal-period close approval it can never execute, taking the policy advisory lock and learning whether an approvalId is usable — but it is the exact rule the dry-run parity memory states. Needs the guard plus a test that fails without it; not committed as an untested guard.
- AFS round 3 (`186f47f92..79ac99a73`) reviewed blind: NOT CLEAN, and the worst finding is MINE. I had reversed the management-pack gate from "refuse to generate an unreviewed pack" to "generate a draft and do not deliver, with a visible reason", because refusing generation would have silently stopped those companies' packs forever. It was implemented as a RETURN VALUE: `deliveryWithheldReason` is written by the generator and the scheduler summary and read nowhere, `deliverPack` has no gate, and `management-packs/[packId]/deliver.ts:34` accepts any pack whose status is 'draft' or 'signed_off' with no review check — so an unreviewed pack is emailed, PDF attached, to every recipient set to 'draft' or 'both'. Round 2 at least left no row to deliver; my reversal created one and left the door open. I confirmed it myself at the named commit before routing it. Fix directed: persist the withheld state on the document and refuse INSIDE deliverPack, the one place every caller passes; the route check is defence in depth. Discovered while confirming: deliverPack has exactly one caller and the scheduler never calls it, so scheduled delivery is dead code today — that is Hein's call to make, not ours to quietly wire up.
- Second P1, same class as the guard-with-no-test lesson: `afsManagementPackGate.int.test.ts` asserts `deliverPack` was not called, but the generator never calls it in any branch, so deleting the entire reviewRequired branch leaves all three assertions passing. A mutant that ADDS a call being caught proves nothing about the delivery route that actually exists.
- The round's own headline fix is right one level down and wrong one level up: `documentContentFingerprint` hashes afs_sections only, while the render also reads the afs_documents row, companies.name, companies.registration_number and the brand profile — change the registered name after the send and the webhook check still passes. Also: `signing_payload_sha256` is written and never read by anything, so "the signature attests exactly what was sent" is true of the section rows, not of the artifact; reorder still permits duplicate note numbers because the schema rejects duplicate sort orders but not duplicate ids; the quarantine reason is written unconditionally even when the download failed, telling an auditor an artifact is on disk that was never written, and it renders the absolute server path to users; and a tenant with no brand_profiles row may now be unable to send for signature at all (mirror direction — establish first whether a profile is guaranteed, in the synthetic fixture only).
- CONTROLS INTEGRATION AFTER THE MCP MERGE (serial, --no-file-parallelism): 1258 passed / 7 failed across 172 files, 515s. Attribution, done properly rather than by calling it load: four are the known pre-existing failures (apSharedWriters reversal binding, auditJobPoller CLI, two auditSnapshotIndexSchema RLS cases); two historicalImportCaseReplay cases and twelve file-level "failures" are DELIBERATE OPT-IN REFUSALS for env I did not set (ISAFLOW_HISTORICAL_CASE_BASELINE, the evidence-scale lease, the isolated-process and bounded-scale opt-ins) — those suites refuse to run rather than pass silently, which is the behaviour we want. The T2c agent independently reached the same conclusion by re-running its suspects in a throwaway worktree at its base commit.
- The seventh was real, and it was the TEST: paymentReleaseUuid's "returns the recorder connection to the pool clean" counted EVERY client backend on the fixture database, excluding only its own two pids. The fixture DB is shared with every other suite and with the other agents' sessions, so any legitimate connection elsewhere read as our leak — it failed in the full run and passed 12/12 alone. That is a test that would eventually be dismissed as flaky, which is exactly how the statement_timeout P1 it exists to catch would come back. Fixed in `adcabaf58`: both pools carry a per-run application_name (PGAPPNAME, which pg's defaults read, so the lazily-created application pool picks it up without changing production pool options) and the count is scoped to it. PROVEN still lethal: restoring statement_timeout 30s above the 6s client query_timeout fails this test and only this test.
- T2c accepted at `60794ad8f`: A20 equality proven field-by-field against the sync detector over a 1086-row population with 150 findings and two reader pages, the FETCH-boundary straddle, the A21 unknown path with a live recount, seal/reader tamper and census gates, and four fence gates including a 276.8s triple-lease run. Seven mutations, all killed and reverted. Confirmed to the agent: A15's vendored pre-seam reference and the >10000-row differential move to T2e with the scale fixture, A17's statement-count gate is an M3 measurement and moves there too, and G2 stays an emulation as long as the evidence doc says so in those words. T2d (transport) dispatched.
- AFS round 4 at `938ae2fa8`. The gate I got wrong is now a gate: `delivery_withheld_reason` (migration 683) is written on the document at generation and `deliverPack` refuses while it is set — I verified that placement myself at the named commit, including that `fetchDocument` selects the column. Only `signOffDocument` clears it, writing an audit row; the route keeps a pre-check as defence in depth. The agent confirmed independently what I found: `deliverPack` has exactly one caller and the scheduler never calls it, so scheduled delivery is dead code today; it did NOT wire it up, which is right — that is Hein's decision.
- Two answers worth keeping. `signing_payload_sha256` is now DECLARED EVIDENCE, not a control, in the code and the evidence doc: a signed, annotated provider PDF can never byte-match posted HTML, so nothing can verify one against the other, and the claim "the signature attests exactly what was sent" is scoped to the row fingerprint — which now covers the document row, companies.name, companies.registration_number and the brand profile, so renaming the company after a send no longer passes the webhook check. And the mirror-direction check paid: NO creation path writes a brand profile, and 19,224 of 19,244 companies IN THE SYNTHETIC FIXTURE have none, so requiring one for signing would have made signing impossible for nearly every tenant. Signing falls back to an exported DEFAULT_BRAND_PROFILE with the fingerprint recording which was used; the PDF export still requires the firm's own branding.
- Round 4 is under blind review (`79ac99a73..938ae2fa8`) before it merges; AFS merges after platform, which is already in. Two documented mutant survivors go to the reviewer with the round: the route's redundant pre-check, and the read-under-lock ordering.
- AFS round 4 review: not clean, narrower. The one that matters, confirmed by me at the commit: migration 683 re-stamps every in-flight signing fingerprint unconditionally (682 had `signing_content_fingerprint IS NULL`; 683 dropped it), so a re-run after go-live overwrites a send-time fingerprint with a hash of the rows as they stand now and launders the exact post-send change the check exists to catch. Directed fix, chosen because it is exact rather than because it is small: re-stamp a row ONLY where its stored value equals the previous formula recomputed over the current rows — that equality means "unchanged since the send", which is exactly when re-stamping is safe. Folded into the same formula change: the pre-image was ambiguous (concat_ws with no escaping, and it drops NULLs, so `Acme|1998/012345/07` with a cleared registration number hashes identically to `Acme` + that number).
- Decided on the main thread, against the obvious fix: company-settings and brand-profile edits will NOT be refused while a document is out for signature. Both are now hashed inputs with no write guard, but blocking a company's own settings for a signing round is the mirror defect, and the signature check already refuses the stale signature. They get a visible warning naming the document the change will invalidate, and the refusal will name WHICH input changed (per-input digests recorded at send) instead of always blaming sections.
- The reviewer settled the gate's scope with evidence, not preference: the plan-7 design doc, the recipient schema, the config UI's Draft/Final/Both select and the Deliver button all show that sending a `draft` to draft-tier recipients on a review_required=TRUE company is deliberate. So the gate is correctly narrow and the comments overclaim — they get corrected to "review SKIPPED". The same evidence surfaced a behaviour change I had not named: the original design for review_required=FALSE was auto-sign-off AND auto-deliver; this branch makes it generate-and-hold. And a real P3: delivered packs carry no DRAFT watermark whatever their status, so a draft-tier recipient gets an unreviewed pack that looks signed. For Hein, not for us: the config UI defaults every new recipient to 'both', so by default everyone receives the unreviewed draft.
- Check-run T2d accepted at `f5f09286d`: three MCP tools (run_control_checks, control_check_run, control_check_run_findings) and the REST endpoint, inventory 163 -> 166. Two practices worth keeping as the standard: the agent caught a refusal test that passed with NO tools registered (an unknown tool is also an error) and added a positive check before committing, and it DELETED a permission hook a mutation proved inert (the registry already checks read:reports) rather than keeping it as decoration. Plan amendment A24 records that the approval_rules trigger accepts one aggregate shape only, so the journal-risk check's "unsupported policy" marker fires in production through that shape, a non-amount/total condition field, or an 'any' operator — and that how many real companies hold such a policy is NOT measured and will not be measured against production. MERGE NOTE for later: this branch and the merged MCP census both move the tool-count assertions and the inventory, so the check-run merge will conflict there; contracts.int.test.ts already fails at both commits on hardcoded counts and a missing local origin, predating both.
- T2e dispatched with A15 (pre-seam reference + >10000-row differential), A17 (MEASURED statement count on the 100k fixture) and G2 (real overrun or keep the word "emulation") moved into its definition of done, and hard-kill defined as a real SIGKILL mid-stream proven by outcome. T2c+T2d under blind review from named commits in parallel.
- T2c+T2d blind review (`bcf5aab53..f5f09286d`): no P1, three P2, six P3 — and one of the P2s contradicts an accepted claim. The T2c report called fence gate G3 "genuine: the lease expires while the transaction streams". I confirmed at the commit that it does not: the test sleeps 10s BEFORE calling runAuditCheckRunPhase, so the phase's FIRST query already sees an expired lease and throws, and the test's regex matches that message too. Deleting the lease predicate from the publication UPDATE survives the suite. I accepted T2c on the report's description of that gate without reading it; the reviewer read it. The publication fence is currently unproven by any gate.
- The other two P2s change limits T2e was about to measure, so the fix round goes first. maxSlabRows counts rows the detector can never group, and the population is cumulative, so one historical day with >1000 journals by one user makes every future run `unknown` for good — isaflow_dev has a 927-row slab for one user on 2026-07-05, which puts the plan's "real slabs <= 6 rows" premise out by two orders of magnitude (dev data; production not checked, and will not be). And `run_control_checks` was write:true behind read:reports only, with a 100-run absolute cap and no retention, so a read-only PAT could lock a tenant out of check runs permanently. Decided: write:audit on both transports, and a rolling-window cap that bounds the rate and cannot become a lockout — which means storage grows without bound until retention exists, a dependency to surface to Hein, not hide behind a cap.
- AFS round 5 at `cbdf89a73`. Migration 683 now re-stamps only where the stored fingerprint equals the 682 formula recomputed over the current rows — verified by me at the commit: a changed document keeps its send-time value and is refused, and a second run cannot match a row already stamped with the new formula. The agent corrected MY instruction with a measurement: the collision I named ('Acme' + number vs 'Acme|number' + NULL) did NOT collide under the old formula, because every nullable field was already COALESCEd; a MOVED separator did ('Acme|1998' + '012345/07' vs 'Acme' + '1998|012345/07' hashed identically, RED). Both are tests now, and the pre-image is jsonb text built from one template shared by service and migration. It also withdrew its own round-4 survivor argument: the Q11 mutant was mis-built (it added an early read but still decided on the locked one, so it could never fail); the corrected mutant moves the deciding read and a two-connection test kills it. Refusals now name the input that changed from per-input digests recorded at send; company and brand edits during a signing round are allowed and return warnings naming each signature they will void; every non-signed-off pack is watermarked, tested on the delivered PDF bytes. Deploy note carried in 683's header: rolling the CODE back with 683 applied fails open. Round 5 under blind review; AFS merges into integration after it clears.
- AFS round 5 review: one P2, confirmed by me at the commit — send-for-signing renders (~:109) before it fingerprints (~:129), and the render reads company and brand in separate READ COMMITTED statements with no lock, so a branding or registration edit inside that window is recorded in the fingerprint while the OLD values are what Signum receives; the webhook then accepts a signature over content the database no longer holds. Fix directed as two halves because one is not enough: FOR SHARE on the company and brand rows before rendering, and a fingerprint before AND after the render with the send refused on any difference — a row lock cannot stop the INSERT of a first brand profile, the comparison can.
- REVERSED MY OWN ROUND-3 DECISION on evidence. I had asked for in-flight signatures to be grandfathered. The reviewer showed from origin/master's code that "sent for signature before this deploy" cannot be produced through the product: master's send reads a stored HTML file nothing on master writes and returns 400 before the only writer of signing_requested_at can run. So 682's and 683's backfills guard a state reachable only by hand, and they are the one mechanism in the round that CAN launder a change (682's formula never covered the document, company or brand, and neither backfill checks section status — which is why the author's N18 survivor argument fails). Both backfill UPDATEs go; the schema stays; a NULL fingerprint already fails closed, which is the right outcome for a hand-made in-flight state. Same round: final-tier recipients were receiving in_review and partner_review packs because sendToRecipient treats only 'draft' as a draft (pre-existing, same delivery path, now in scope), and eight rendered inputs plus section_type are either untested or absent from the pre-image.
- AFS round 6 at `bb2804907` closed the send-time race with FOR SHARE on the company and brand rows plus a before/after fingerprint comparison; grandfather backfills removed (682/683 now only add columns; a NULL fingerprint is refused at the webhook with a re-send instruction); final-tier recipients no longer receive in_review/partner_review packs; nine rendered inputs each have a per-field test and section_type joined the pre-image; the N18 survivor is withdrawn with a test. 158 AFS integration passed. NOT ACCEPTED as-is, on the author's own stated trade-off: "a company or branding save waits up to ~30s". I counted the writers of the companies row at the commit — logo, chase settings, PIS score, CIPC annual return, the client query WhatsApp number, the PERIOD-LOCK control, three trial transitions, four admin paths, bulk actions, PAYG eligibility — and a FOR SHARE row lock blocks every one of them during a send. Unrelated features were paying to protect two columns. Round 7 directed: fingerprint what you rendered — one statement returns the company and brand inputs AND their digests, the render uses those values, so the fingerprint is over the signed bytes by construction and any later change refuses at the webhook. No lock, no comparison, and a test proving an unrelated companies UPDATE during a send completes promptly.
- AFS round 7 at `c66747ed9`: the send fingerprints what it renders. `snapshotRenderInputs` reads the company and brand inputs and their digests in ONE statement and the render takes those values through `renderDocumentHtml({ inputs })`; verified at the commit that the signing path passes the snapshot and that no FOR SHARE on companies or brand_profiles remains. The three second-connection tests all went RED against round 6's locks first ("a chase-settings save waited" among them), and four mutants (render re-reads brand, render re-reads company, either lock restored) are killed. Rounds 6-7 (`cbdf89a73..c66747ed9`) under blind review as the last round before AFS merges.
- AFS rounds 6-7 blind review: NO P1, NO P2, clean to merge. The reviewer re-ran the signing and delivery suites itself (43/43) in a detached worktree, built five mutants of its own (all red), traced every rendered value to either the snapshot or the document lock, and confirmed the send and the webhook both fingerprint through snapshotRenderInputs. Its "merge blocker" (≈40 conflicts against origin/master) is the expected shape of a branch stacked on this programme — it merges into integration, not master. Final P3 round dispatched under the fix-all rule: a sort_order tie gives one fingerprint two byte orders; four fields fingerprinted but never rendered void signatures over identical bytes; three stale comments; a test that cannot tell default colours from null ones. Two items go to Hein, not to the code: the logo is fetched by the provider from `logo_url`, so the fingerprint covers the URL and not the image (fix direction: embed and hash the bytes), and the report footer is never printed at all. Mentioned, not deleted: `lockAfsDocument` has no callers and would bypass signing and delivery tiers if ever wired up.
- AFS round 8 (`0cdc05b2f`) removed every fingerprinted field the render never reads — more than I listed, including document/section review status and the signatory — so it got a focused review before merge, because loosening a security fingerprint is where a real defect hides. Sound: each removed field traced through the renderer and confirmed unread; an ACCEPTED AI narrative is still covered because acceptance copies it into `overrides`; a mandatory section losing sign-off is still refused by the webhook's separate count; all five claimed mutants re-run by the reviewer and real. The one P2 is the recurring drift class: the brand-profile warning predicate was a hand-maintained second copy of the fingerprint's field list and still compared the footer, so a footer-only save would tell an operator to cancel a valid signing. Directed: warnings computed from the fingerprint itself (snapshotRenderInputs before and after the write, same transaction) on all three write paths, so no field list exists outside afsSigningIntegrity.ts. For Hein: a SUPPRESSED note is still printed in the signed statements (suppress.ts records a reason; the render never filters on it) — pre-existing, outside this branch.
- AFS -> integration trial merge (merge-tree, non-destructive): ONE conflict, afsRestoreReviewState.int.test.ts, where both sides are right — integration replaced a 400ms timer with a wait until the restore is genuinely blocked, AFS changed restoreVersion to take the document. Resolution planned: AFS's five-argument call inside integration's wait loop.
